{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_vrtx_011ChEm8Ynt4HRkRzLzENuwD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I couldn't watch the clip: `/app/.harbor_assets/` is empty and there's no `recording.mp4` anywhere on the system. So I worked from your list of expected behavior and the layout code that lines up charts' plot areas. I found three bugs there and fixed them.\n\n### Fixes\n1. **Labels on the left and right ended up in the wrong place** (`src/lib/models/layouts/alignments.ts`). When charts were lined up, each chart took its left margin from the wrong column and its right margin from the wrong column too (left and right were swapped). Margins were reserved in the wrong place, so axis labels could be cut off or overlap the next chart.\n2. **The top margin was never applied** (`src/lib/core/layout/border.ts`). The shared left, right and bottom margins were respected, but the top one wasn't. So a chart with a short title or toolbar didn't line up with taller neighbours, and the space came out of its plot area or pushed things outside the chart.\n3. **Rows and columns never lined up their margins at all** (`src/lib/models/layouts/layout_dom.ts`). Only grids did this step; rows, columns and tabs with `link_layouts` skipped it. In a row, lining up the plot areas then ate into each chart's own margins. With a fixed `frame_width` or `frame_height`, axes and toolbars got pushed outside the chart. Without one, the plot area shrank. I moved the step into the base layout view so every container that lines up charts does it, and removed the grid-only copy in `css_grid_box.ts`.\n\n### Checks\n- `node make build` succeeds.\n- The browser tests didn't run because there's no Chrome in this environment, and I haven't seen it working in a browser.\n- Instead I ran a small Node script on the built layout code with two charts side by side, each with a different-sized title and axes. Both now get the same top and bottom margins, so they share a height and their plot areas line up, while each keeps its own left and right margins. So a chart with a short title or toolbar didn't line up with taller neighbours, and the space came out of its plot area or pushed things outside the chart.\n3. **Rows and columns never lined up their margins at all** (`src/lib/models/layouts/layout_dom.ts`). With a fixed `frame_width` or `frame_height`, axes and toolbars got pushed outside the chart. Without one, the plot area shrank.
